We need to prove strong normalization for STLC with CBV semantics, locally nameless, with if-then-else. Standard approach: logical relation / reducibility candidates adapted to CBV.

Let me think about the design.

Language: STLC with Bool, arrow, if. CBV small-step.

We need to show every closed well-typed term is SN.

Standard approach: Define a unary logical predicate on terms, indexed by type:
- For Bool: SN(t) perhaps with additional closure under if? Actually for Bool, we can take the "hereditarily strongly normalizing" or reducibility candidate. Since CBV, a value of Bool type is true or false. But terms of Bool type need not be values.

A clean way: define the logical relation as a "reducibility candidate" in the style of Girard, adapted to CBV, where candidates are sets of terms closed under reduction. Alternatively use the standard "SN + something" approach.

Approach: Define `R T t` (unary logical relation):
- R Bool t := strongly_normalizing t.

Definitions:

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  ...
  end.
```

Actually simplest is to prove all needed lemmas by induction on T:

Lemma sem_sn: sem T t -> SN t. By induction on T.
- Bool: assumption.
- Arrow: sem = SN t ∧ ...; take first component.

Lemma sem_step: sem T t -> t --> t' -> sem T t'. By induction on T.
- Bool: SN t and t --> t'. Need SN t': SN_intro inversion. strongly_normalizing is an inductive with one constructor; inversion on SN t gives forall t'', t-->t'' -> SN t''; apply to t'. Simpler: define a destructor lemma using pattern matching:
```
Lemma SN_inv : forall t, strongly_normalizing t -> forall t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t H. destruct H as [t Hred]. exact (Hred). Qed.
```

- Bool: SN of true/false: destruct v; true/false have no steps (need to prove no step from tm_true; inversion on step). v_abs is not Bool-typed semantically but for Bool case we just need any value is SN: true, false, abs. We need "backward closure" / "expanded" closure: if t --> t' and sem T t' then sem T t. In Girard's reducibility, candidates are closed under reduction (forward) and often also expansion (if all reducts are in the set, then the term is). Known approach (from Martin-Löf / Tait): Define computability for CBV:
- A term of base type is computable iff SN.
- A term of arrow type is computable iff SN and for all computable values v of the argument type, the application is computable.
This is the definition we had. Tait's proof of the fundamental theorem uses the fact that for a variable or neutral, computability follows from computability of all its reducts (the "CR" closure conditions).

```
Definition halts (t : tm) : Prop := exists t', t -->* t' /\ value t'.

Definition R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => halts t
  | T1 -> T2 => forall t', R T1 t' -> R T2 (app t t')
  end.
```
That's weak normalization (halts). For strong normalization, more is needed.

I think I'm overcomplicating. Let's recall Girard's proof structure properly:

Define candidates as sets of SN terms closed under reduction and neutral expansion. The interpretation ⟦T⟧ is a candidate (proved by induction on T). Then prove:
(I) Variables are in ⟦T⟧ under any valuation of type-consistent variables. More generally the fundamental theorem.
(II) Adequacy: ⟦T⟧ ⊆ SN — this is part of the candidate definition (condition 1), so no separate structural induction needed! Because ⟦T⟧ is *proven* to be a candidate, which includes SN. How is condition 1 proven for arrow? That's the crux: ⟦T1→T2⟧ ⊆ SN. Standard proof: Let t ∈ ⟦T1→T2⟧. Show SN t. Prove the auxiliary statement: for any term t ∈ ⟦T1→T2⟧, and any argument... Hmm, consider the reduction tree of t. If t is a value abs, SN. If t non-value, then t's one-step reducts t' are in ⟦T1→T2⟧ by closure under reduction (condition 2 of arrow candidate). To show SN t, by SN_intro need SN t' for all reducts. So we need: all reducts of t are SN. But that's exactly "∀ t' ∈ Red(t), SN t'" which holds if ⟦arrow⟧ ⊆ SN... circular.

The standard way out: In Girard's proof, SN is defined not inductively but as "no infinite reduction sequence", and the candidate condition "C ⊆ SN" is proven for the arrow candidate via: suppose t ∈ arrow and t is not SN, then there's an infinite reduction sequence t = t0 --> t1 --> ...; all ti ∈ arrow (closure). If infinitely many steps happen "at the head"... you can construct an infinite sequence by applying to some argument v ∈ ⟦T1⟧? That requires t to be applied; but the reductions might be inside arguments. Hmm, for CBV with values, the standard proof uses a "sandwich" lemma.

So we need a more general backward-closure lemma: "if t --> t', SN t, ⟦T⟧ t', and t' is the unique reduct of t (or all reducts of t are in ⟦T⟧), then ⟦T⟧ t". The neutral expansion lemma requires ~value t. Here t = app (app abs v) w is an application → not a value ✓. All reducts of t:

The lemma statement requires SN t as a hypothesis, and we can prove SN t separately because "all reducts are SN" is exactly the SN_intro rule: SN t := forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'. So if all reducts t' are in ⟦T⟧, then by adequacy (IH on T) each t' is SN, hence SN t by SN_intro. No circularity, as long as adequacy of T is proven first by induction on T. But adequacy of arrow T = A→B: ⟦A→B⟧ t = SN t ∧ ..., so it's immediate, no need for app clause! Great. Now, what other closure properties do we need?

1. sem_step (forward closure): ⟦T⟧ t → t --> t' → ⟦T⟧ t'. Induction on T.
   - Bool: SN t' from SN_inv ✓.
   - Arrow: SN t' from SN_inv. App clause: v value ⟦A⟧ v: need ⟦B⟧ (t' v). We have ⟦B⟧ (t v). And t v --> t' v by ST_App1 (lc v ✓). By IH_B forward closure, ⟦B⟧ (t' v) ✓.

So the fundamental theorem statement:

```
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> sem T (sigma x).

Definition env_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    env_lc sigma ->
    env_rel Gamma sigma ->
    sem T (subst sigma t) /\ locally_closed (subst sigma t).
```

```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) :=
  match L with [] => 0 | a :: L' => Nat.max a (max_list L') end.
Lemma in_max_list : forall L a, In a L -> a <= max_list L.
Lemma max_list_lt_S : forall L, forall a, In a L -> a < S (max_list L).
Lemma fresh_atom : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof. intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intros H. apply in_max_list in H. lia. Qed.

Then inversion gives it. Let's define:

```
Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.
```
Then `fvs (abs T t1) = fvs t1` definitionally; `~ In x (fvs (abs T t1))` gives ~ In x (fvs t1) by unfolding (cbn).    Lemma sem_app_aux : forall t, SN t ->

So sem definition:
```
Fixpoint sem (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t /\
      (forall v, value v -> sem T1 v -> sem T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```
Note: `sem` is a Fixpoint on T; the recursive calls are on subterms, structural ✓. Unary logical relation.

  assert (Hxnot : ~ In x (fvs t1)) by (intro H; apply Hfresh; apply in_app_iff; right; exact H).
  ...
  ```
  Then use x throughout.

For the env_rel extension, let's write a helper lemma:
```
Lemma env_rel_update : forall Gamma x T1 sigma v,
  env_rel Gamma sigma -> sem T1 v ->
  env_rel (update Gamma x T1) (fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y).
Proof.
  intros Gamma x T1 sigma v Henv Hv y T Hy.
  unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hy as ->. exact Hv.  (* Hy : Some T1 = Some T *)
  - exact (Henv y T Hy).
Qed.
```

W. Freshness: We need `fresh_atom : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L`. Using max_list.

Let me write:
```
Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | [] => 0
  | a :: L' => Nat.max a (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma in_max_list : forall L a, In a L -> a <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| b L IH]; intros a Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl. destruct Hin as [->|Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list L); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intros Hin.
  pose proof (in_max_list L _ Hin). lia.
Qed.
```
Requires lia. Let's Require Import Lia (stdlib). Or use Nat.lt_succ_r: `apply Nat.lt_succ_r in ...`. `in_max_list` gives `S (max_list L) <= max_list L`, which is absurd; `lia` handles. Or `exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l ...)`? Let's just use lia; it's stdlib.

Actually, the task file imports Arith.PeanoNat, Lists.List, Relation_Definitions. We can add `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` and `List.` (for in_app_iff). AB. step_lc_preserve:

Induction on step derivation. Cases:
- ST_AppAbs T t v Hlcabs Hval: goal lc (open t v). We have Hlcabs : lc (abs T t), Hval : value v. Need lc (open_rec 0 v t). Use lc_open_rec: lc_at 1 t (from Hlcabs by inversion: lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t) → lc_at 1 t) and lc_at 0 v (value_lc Hval). ✓.
- ST_App1 t1 t1' t2 Hstep Hlc2: goal lc (app t1' t2). Inversion on the whole lc (app t1 t2) gives lc t1 and lc t2; IH Hstep lc_t1 gives lc t1'. constructor ✓.
  But wait: the induction principle on step: our statement is `forall t t', step t t' -> lc t -> lc t'`. Doing `induction H` with H : step t t' generalizes? The step constructors have recursive premises (ST_App1's step t1 t1'); the IH will be `lc t1 -> lc t1'`? - ST_App2 v1 t2 t2' Hval Hstep: goal lc (app v1 t2'); need lc v1 (value_lc Hval) and lc t2' (IH Hstep with lc t2 from inversion of Hlc).

- ST_IfFalse ✓.
- ST_If t1 t1' t2 t3 Hstep Hlc2 Hlc3: goal lc (if t1' t2 t3); need lc t1' (IH with lc t1 from inversion), lc t2, lc t3 ✓.
✓.

AD. sem_step (forward closure):
```
Lemma sem_step : forall T t t', sem T t -> step t t' -> sem T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' Hsem Hst.
  - destruct Hsem as [Hsn Hlc]. split.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
  - destruct Hsem as [Hsn [Hlc Happ]].
    split; [| split].
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply step_lc with (t := t); assumption.
    + intros v Hv Hsemv.
      apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t v).
      * apply Happ; assumption.
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply value_lc; exact Hv].
Qed.
```
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2. So `apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply value_lc; exact Hv]`. ✓.

AE. sem_expand:
```
Lemma sem_expand : forall T t,
  ~ value t ->
  locally_closed t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> sem T t') ->
  sem T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hnv Hlc Hred.
  - split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. destruct (Hred t' Hst) as [Hsn _]. exact Hsn.
    + exact Hlc.
  - split; [| split].
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. destruct (Hred t' Hst) as [Hsn _]. exact Hsn.
    + exact Hlc.
    + intros v Hv Hsemv.
      apply IH2.
      * intro Hvapp. inversion Hvapp.
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply sem_lc with (T := T1); exact Hsemv].
      * intros t' Hst. inversion Hst as [T0 t0 v0 Hlcabs Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hstep Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hstep]; subst.
        -- (* ST_AppAbs: t = tm_abs T0 t0, v0 = v (the arg), t' = open t0 v0 *)
           exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. exact Hlc.
           (* Hmm: after inversion, t has been replaced by tm_abs T0 t0 and Hlc has type lc (tm_abs T0 t0) presumably; but Hlc was for the original t. Inversion/subst should handle it. But careful: Hnv : ~value t; after subst t := tm_abs T0 t0, Hnv : ~value (tm_abs T0 t0); apply Hnv; apply v_abs; exact Hlc ✓ (need Hlc : lc (tm_abs T0 t0)). *)
        -- (* ST_App1: t = t1, t1' = ..., t2 = v; t' = tm_app t1' v *) 
           (* Hred t1' Hstep : sem (T1→T2) t1'. Apply app clause with v. *)
           destruct (Hred t1' Hstep) as [Hsn [Hlc' Happ]]. apply Happ; assumption.
        -- (* ST_App2: value v1 where v1 = t; contradiction *)
           exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hval.
    ...
```
Careful with inversion pattern names. The step constructors:
```
ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, lc (abs T t) -> value v -> tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> lc t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2
ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'
ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1
ST_IfFalse : forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> tm_if tm_false t1 t2 --> t2
ST_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3, t1 --> t1' -> lc t2 -> lc t3 -> tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> tm_if t1' t2 t3
```
Inversion on Hst : step (tm_app t v) t' should yield the app cases only; the if constructors can't unify (tm_app vs tm_if). Good. Also note in ST_App1, the conclusion is tm_app t1 t2 with t1 = t and t2 = v; t' = tm_app t1' v.

But there's a catch: inversion with names — the pattern syntax `inversion Hst as [T0 t0 v0 Hlcabs Hval | ...]` requires exactly matching constructor argument counts; the constructor's arguments get named. Since some premises are hypotheses, inversion names them. Let's not rely on exact naming; we can do `inversion Hst; subst` and then handle goals with `try` and `auto`. Let's think about how many goals inversion produces: only ST_AppAbs, ST_App1, ST_App2 (the if constructors are eliminated by discrimination, I believe inversion will not produce goals for impossible constructors). Let's write:

```
inversion Hst; subst.
```
and then 3 goals. In each, the variables from the constructor are introduced by inversion with some names (Coq generates fresh names like T0, t0, v0, H1, H2...). Using `inversion Hst; subst` might not name them deterministically. Better to use `inversion Hst as [...]` with explicit patterns and adapt after seeing errors. Alternatively, use `dependent destruction`? No.

Alternatively, prove dedicated inversion lemmas for step:
```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 t',
  step (tm_app t1 t2) t' ->
  (exists T t v, t1 = tm_abs T t /\ t2 = v /\ t' = open t v) \/
  (exists t1', step t1 t1' /\ t' = tm_app t1' t2) \/
  (exists v1 t2', t1 = v1 /\ value v1 /\ step t2 t2' /\ t' = tm_app v1 t2').
```
Hmm, writing these inversion lemmas by hand is doable. But maybe just use `inversion Hst; subst; ...` and let Coq name things; we can inspect the goal in the error messages iteratively. I'll iterate.

AF. sem_app. Let's write it carefully.

```
Lemma sem_app_aux : forall t,
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall T1 T2, sem (T1 → T2) t -> sem T1 u -> sem T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof.
  intros t Hsn_t.
  induction Hsn_t as [t Hred_t IH_t].
  intros u Hsn_u.

AG. sem_if:
```
Lemma sem_if : forall T b1 b2 b3,
  strongly_normalizing b1 ->
  locally_closed b1 ->
  sem T b2 -> sem T b3 ->
  locally_closed b2 -> locally_closed b3 ->
  sem T (tm_if b1 b2 b3).
Proof.
  intros T b1 b2 b3 Hsn.
  revert T b2 b3.    (* hmm, order *)
```
Let me state it as:
```
Lemma sem_if : forall b1, strongly_normalizing b1 -> forall T b2 b3,
  locally_closed b1 -> sem T b2 -> sem T b3 -> locally_closed b2 -> locally_closed b3 ->
  sem T (tm_if b1 b2 b3).
Proof.
  intros b1 Hsn. induction Hsn as [b1 Hred IH].
  intros T b2 b3 Hlc1 Hsem2 Hsem3 Hlc2 Hlc3.
  apply sem_expand with (t := tm_if b1 b2 b3).
  - intro Hv; inversion Hv.
  - apply lc_if; assumption.
  - intros t' Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
    + (* ST_IfTrue: b1 = tm_true, t' = b2 *) assumption.
    + (* ST_IfFalse: b1 = tm_false, t' = b3 *) assumption.
    + (* ST_If: b1 --> t1', t' = tm_if t1' b2 b3 *) 
      apply IH.
      * exact H.  (* step b1 t1' *)
      * apply step_lc with (t := b1); [exact H | exact Hlc1].
      * assumption. ... 
```
Hmm, the IH from induction on Hsn: IH : forall b1', step b1 b1' -> forall T b2 b3, lc b1' -> sem T b2 -> sem T b3 -> lc b2 -> lc b3 -> sem T (if b1' b2 b3). Wait, the induction motive: after `intros b1 Hsn`, the goal is `forall T b2 b3, lc b1 -> sem T b2 -> ...`. Hmm, the `lc b1` depends on b1; if it's after the intros, the motive P(b1) = forall T b2 b3, lc b1 -> ... ✓ (lc b1 is a premise in the goal, fine).

Actually the order: Lemma sem_if : forall b1, SN b1 -> forall T b2 b3, lc b1 -> sem T b2 -> sem T b3 -> lc b2 -> lc b3 -> sem T (if ...). After intros b1 Hsn, goal: forall T b2 b3, lc b1 -> ... (Hsn reverted? no, Hsn is in context). induction Hsn as [b1 Hred IH] — the motive is fun b1 => (forall T b2 b3, lc b1 -> ...). But Hlc1 is inside the motive. Then IH : forall b1', step b1 b1' -> forall T b2 b3, lc b1' -> sem T b2 -> sem T b3 -> lc b2 -> lc b3 -> sem T (if b1' b2 b3) ✓. 

Then after induction, `intros T b2 b3 Hlc1 Hsem2 Hsem3 Hlc2 Hlc3`. ✓.

ST_If case: need apply IH with b1' = t1', step b1 t1' (from the step premise H : step b1 t1'? inversion gives the recursive step as a hypothesis), lc t1' (step_lc H Hlc1), sem T b2, sem T b3, lc b2, lc b3. ✓.

AH. sem_var:
```
Lemma sem_var : forall T x, sem T (tm_fvar x).
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros x.
  - split; [apply SN_fvar | apply lc_fvar].
  - split; [apply SN_fvar | split; [apply lc_fvar |]].
    intros v Hval Hsemv.
    apply sem_expand with (t := tm_app (tm_fvar x) v).
    + intro Hv; inversion Hv.
    + apply lc_app; [apply lc_fvar | apply sem_lc with (T := T1); exact Hsemv].
    + intros t' Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
      * (* AppAbs: fvar x = abs *) inversion H. (* H : fvar x = tm_abs ...? inversion on the equality or on Hval? *) 
      * (* App1: step (fvar x) t1' *) inversion H.
      * (* App2: value (fvar x) *) inversion H.
Qed.
```
In the AppAbs case, the inversion gives an equation fvar x = tm_abs T0 body (or directly replaces), and/or the hypothesis value v. Actually ST_AppAbs requires t1 = tm_abs T t, where t1 = tm_fvar x; unification should fail (fvar ≠ abs), so inversion might close that case automatically. Similarly App2 requires value (tm_fvar x) which is not derivable, but it's a hypothesis of the constructor; inversion produces Hval : value (tm_fvar x) and we can `inversion Hval`. App1 produces Hstep : step (tm_fvar x) t1' and we can `inversion Hstep`.

AI. lc part of T_Abs: we need `lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1)`. Detailed proof:
```
(* Choose x fresh from L ++ fvs t1 *)
destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvs t1)) as [x Hx].
assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro H; apply Hx, in_app_iff; left; exact H).
assert (Hxf : ~ In x (fvs t1)) by (intro H; apply Hx, in_app_iff; right; exact H).
set (sigma0 := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then tm_fvar x else sigma y).
assert (Hlc0 : env_lc sigma0).
  { intro y. unfold sigma0. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [apply lc_fvar | apply Hlc]. }
assert (Henv0 : env_rel (update Gamma x T1) sigma0).
  { apply env_rel_update; [exact Henv | apply sem_var]. }
pose proof (IHpre x HxL sigma0 Hlc0 Henv0) as Hsem_body.
(* Hsem_body : sem T2 (subst sigma0 (open t1 (fvar x))) *)
assert (Hopen_eq : subst sigma0 (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) (subst sigma t1)).
  { apply subst_open_rec; [exact Hlc | exact Hxf]. }
(* Hmm, subst_open_rec's LHS uses `fun y => if eqb x y then v else sigma y` with v := tm_fvar x, which is exactly sigma0 if we unfold. *)
rewrite Hopen_eq in Hsem_body.
apply sem_lc in Hsem_body.
(* Hsem_body : lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) (subst sigma t1)) *)
apply (lc_open_rec_inv (subst sigma t1) 0 (tm_fvar x)) in Hsem_body; [exact Hsem_body | apply lc_fvar].
```

AN. Fundamental T_Abs sem app clause: We need to apply sem_expand at T2. Let's detail:
```
(* goal: sem T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) v) *)
apply (sem_expand T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) v)).
- intro Hv; inversion Hv.
- apply lc_app; [apply lc_abs; exact Hlc_body | apply value_lc; exact Hval].

  + (* ST_AppAbs T0 t0 v0 Hlcabs Hval0: t = tm_abs T0 t0 → unify: T0 = T1, t0 = body, v0 = v; t' = open t0 v0 = open_rec 0 body v *)
    (* Need sem T2 (open_rec 0 (subst sigma t1) v) *)
    (* Have IHpre x HxL sigma' Hlc' Henv' : sem T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))) *)
    rewrite <- Hopen_eq' ... 
  + (* ST_App1: step (tm_abs T1 body) t1' — impossible *) 
    exfalso; apply (abs_no_step ...); exact H.
  + (* ST_App2: value (abs) ✓ and step v t2' — impossible since v value *)
    exfalso; apply (value_no_step v Hval); exact H.
```

AQ. The `sem` Fixpoint with two arguments and recursion on T. Since sem is defined by recursion on ty with a tm argument: 
```
Fixpoint sem (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t /\
      (forall v, value v -> sem T1 v -> sem T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```
Structural recursion on T ✓ (calls sem T1 v and sem T2 ... on subterms). Coq accepts.

AR. env_rel / env_lc definitions:
```
Definition env_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> sem T (sigma x).
```

Then inside the module after the strongly_normalizing inductive (line 224), insert:

```coq
(** ***** Supporting definitions for the logical relation. *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.
```

Freshness:
```coq
Fixpoint max_list (L : list nat) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | a :: L' => Nat.max a (max_list L')
  end.

Lemma in_max_list : forall L a, In a L -> a <= max_list L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| b L IH]; intros a Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list L); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). intros Hin.
  pose proof (in_max_list L (S (max_list L)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

Lemma lc_at_S : forall k t, lc_at k t -> lc_at (S k) t.

  intros k t H. induction H.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; assumption.
Qed.

```coq
  intros k k' t Hle Ht. induction Hle.

Qed.

Let me add inversion lemmas for lc_at to make life easier:
```coq
Lemma lc_bvar_inv : forall k i, lc_at k (tm_bvar i) -> i < k.
Proof. intros k i H. inversion H. assumption. Qed.
Lemma lc_app_inv : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
Proof. intros. inversion H. split; assumption. Qed.

```

Lemma step_lc : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof. *)

(** Free-variable substitution (locally nameless: fvars are substituted,
    bvars are untouched). *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

(** The unary logical relation.  At every type we require strong
    normalization and local closure; at arrow types we additionally
    require that application to any related value is related. *)

Fixpoint sem (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t /\
      (forall v, value v -> sem T1 v -> sem T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.

Definition env_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).

Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> sem T (sigma x).
```

Then lemmas.

I need to double-check: is `sem` accepted as a Fixpoint with two args? Yes: `Fixpoint sem (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := match T with ... sem T1 v ...`. The recursive calls are on T1/T2 which are subterms of T. But Coq's guard checker: in the body, `sem T1 v` and `sem T2 (tm_app t v)` — the decreasing argument is T, T1/T2 are subterms ✓. However, `match T with | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ...` — the recursive call on T1 is fine.

Potential issue: `sem`'s second argument t changes; the guard condition allows that as long as the first decreases. ✓.

(** ** Basic facts about values, steps, and strong normalization. *)

Lemma value_lc : forall v, value v -> locally_closed v.
Proof.
  intros v H. destruct H as [T t Hlc | | ].
  - exact Hlc.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma value_no_step : forall v v', value v -> ~ step v v'.
Proof.
  intros v v' Hv Hst. destruct Hv as [T t Hlc | | ]; inversion Hst.
Qed.

Lemma abs_no_step : forall T t u, ~ step (tm_abs T t) u.
Proof. intros T t u H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_abs : forall T t, strongly_normalizing (tm_abs T t).
Proof. intros T t. apply SN_intro. intros u H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_true : strongly_normalizing tm_true.
Proof. apply SN_intro. intros u H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_false : strongly_normalizing tm_false.
Proof. apply SN_intro. intros u H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_fvar : forall x, strongly_normalizing (tm_fvar x).
Proof. intros x. apply SN_intro. intros u H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' H Hst. destruct H as [t0 Hred]. apply (Hred t' Hst). Qed.

(** ** Projections of the logical relation. *)

Lemma sem_sn : forall T t, sem T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in H; apply (proj1 H).

Lemma sem_lc : forall T t, sem T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T as [| T1 T2]; simpl in H.
  - apply (proj2 H).

Qed.

  forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall T1 T2, sem (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> sem T1 u -> sem T2 (tm_app t u).

Lemma sem_if : forall b1, strongly_normalizing b1 ->

Let me now write the fundamental theorem proof.

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, env_lc sigma -> env_rel Gamma sigma -> sem T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [Gamma x T Hx
                | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hpre IHpre
                | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IHt1 Ht2 IHt2
                | Gamma
                | Gamma
                | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T Ht1 IHt1 Ht2 IHt2 Ht3 IHt3].
  - (* T_Var *) intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl. apply (Henv x T Hx).
  - (* T_Abs *)
    intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl.
    (* goal: sem (T1 -> T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) *)
    split.
    + apply SN_abs.
    + split.
      * (* local closure of the abstraction *)
        apply lc_abs.
        (* need lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1) *)
        ...
      * (* application clause *)
        ...
  - (* T_App *)
    intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl.
    apply (sem_app_aux (subst sigma t1) (sem_sn (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (IHt1 sigma Hlc Henv))
                       (subst sigma t2) (sem_sn T1 (IHt2 sigma Hlc Henv)) T1 T2).
    + exact (IHt1 sigma Hlc Henv).
    + exact (IHt2 sigma Hlc Henv).
  - (* T_True *) intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl. split; [apply SN_true | apply lc_true].
  - (* T_False *) intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl. split; [apply SN_false | apply lc_false].
  - (* T_If *)
    intros sigma Hlc Henv. simpl.
    apply (sem_if (subst sigma t1)).
    + apply (sem_sn Ty_Bool). exact (IHt1 sigma Hlc Henv).
    + apply (sem_lc Ty_Bool (subst sigma t1) (IHt1 sigma Hlc Henv)).  (* lc of condition *)
    + exact (IHt2 sigma Hlc Henv).
    + exact (IHt3 sigma Hlc Henv).
    + apply (sem_lc T (subst sigma t2) (IHt2 sigma Hlc Henv)).
    + apply (sem_lc T (subst sigma t3) (IHt3 sigma Hlc Henv)).
Qed.
```

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x)) as Hsem.
  assert (Hlc : env_lc (fun x => tm_fvar x)) by (intro x; apply lc_fvar).
  assert (Henv : env_rel empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { intros x T0 Hc. unfold empty in Hc. discriminate. }
  specialize (Hsem Hlc Henv).
  rewrite subst_id in Hsem.
  apply (sem_sn T t). exact Hsem.
Qed.
```
Careful: `pose proof (fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x)) as Hsem` — the theorem's implicit arguments: fundamental takes Gamma t T then H then sigma then env_lc then env_rel. Applying it to `(fun x => tm_fvar x)` leaves the two env hypotheses as premises, so Hsem : env_lc ... -> env_rel empty ... -> sem T (subst ...). Then specialize.
